.
Accordingly, we can formulate the
counterpart as
{\sf OPT}({\ref{floor}})={\sf OPT}(\text{input})-\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\downarrow}
, using

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\downarrow}=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\big(P^{\downarrow}(v)-P(v)\big)\cdot\mathcal{V}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v=\int_{0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}\big(1-P(v)\big)\cdot\mathcal{V}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v,

•
‣
3.1

and formulate the
counterpart as
{\sf OPT}({\ref{ceiling}})={\sf OPT}(\text{input})+\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\uparrow}
, using

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\uparrow}=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\big(P(v)-P^{\uparrow}(v)\big)\cdot\mathcal{V}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v=\int_{0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}P(v)\cdot\mathcal{V}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v.\hskip 28.45274pt

•
‣
3.1

Notice that all the four terms
\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\downarrow}
etc are nonnegative. The remaining proof relies on
A
. (For brevity, we ignore the “
0/0
” issue, which can happen only if EITHER
P\equiv P^{\downarrow}
OR
P\equiv P^{\uparrow}
, making
Item

2
vacuously true.)

Fact A
.

\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\downarrow}/\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\downarrow}\geq\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\uparrow}/\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\uparrow}
or equivalently,
\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\downarrow}/\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\downarrow}\leq\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\uparrow}/\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\uparrow}
.

Proof.

It is more convenient to verify the second equation: Using the (normalized) antiderivatives

\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(v):=(1/\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\downarrow})\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)\cdot\int_{0}^{v}(1-P(t))\cdot\mathrm{d}t
AND

\mathfrak{P}(v):=(1/\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\uparrow})\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)\cdot\int_{0}^{v}P(t)\cdot\mathrm{d}t
, we can rewrite

{\rm{LHS}}\equiv\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\downarrow}\big/\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\downarrow}=\int_{0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}(v)\cdot\mathcal{V}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v
AND

{\rm{RHS}}\equiv\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\uparrow}/\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\uparrow}=\int_{0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}(v)\cdot\mathcal{V}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v
.

Using integration by parts, we can deduce
A
as follows:

\displaystyle{\rm{LHS}}

\displaystyle~=~\Big(\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(v)\cdot\mathcal{V}(v)\Big)\,\Big|\,\Big._{v=0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}~-~\int_{0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(v)\cdot\mathcal{V}^{\prime}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle~=~\Big(\mathfrak{P}(v)\cdot\mathcal{V}(v)\Big)\,\Big|\,\Big._{v=0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}~-~\int_{0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(v)\cdot\mathcal{V}^{\prime}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v

(P1)

\displaystyle~\leq~\Big(\mathfrak{P}(v)\cdot\mathcal{V}(v)\Big)\,\Big|\,\Big._{v=0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}~-~\int_{0}^{\varphi_{1}(0)}\mathfrak{P}(v)\cdot\mathcal{V}^{\prime}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v~=~{\rm{RHS}}.

(P2)

(
P1
): For the first term, by construction
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(v)=\mathfrak{P}(v)=0
and
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(\varphi_{1}(0))=\mathfrak{P}(\varphi_{1}(0))=1
.

(
P2
): For the second term, by construction
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(v)\geq\mathfrak{P}(v)\geq 0
and the PDF
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}(v)\geq 0
.
In particular, the
antiderivative
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}(v)
is concave
\overline{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime\prime}(v)=-(1/\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\downarrow})\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)\cdot P^{\prime}(v)\leq 0
, whereas the
antiderivative
\mathfrak{P}(v)
is convex
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime\prime}(v)=(1/\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\uparrow})\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)\cdot P^{\prime}(v)\geq 0
.
∎

Assume the opposite to
Item

2
: The
/
candidates
H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}_{-1}\otimes L
and
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}_{-1}\otimes L
EACH yield a strictly larger bound than the input
(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
. That is,

\displaystyle{\sf PoA}({\ref{floor}})~~\,\;>~{\sf PoA}(\text{input})

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\frac{{\sf FPA}(\text{input})-\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\downarrow}}{{\sf OPT}(\text{input})-\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\downarrow}}~>~\frac{{\sf FPA}(\text{input})}{{\sf OPT}(\text{input})},

\displaystyle{\sf PoA}({\ref{ceiling}})~>~{\sf PoA}(\text{input})

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\frac{{\sf FPA}(\text{input})+\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\uparrow}}{{\sf OPT}(\text{input})+\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\uparrow}}~>~\frac{{\sf FPA}(\text{input})}{{\sf OPT}(\text{input})}.

Rearranging both equations gives
\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\downarrow}/\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\downarrow}<{\sf PoA}(\text{input})<\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}^{\uparrow}/\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}^{\uparrow}
, which contradicts
A
.
Refute our assumption: At least one candidate between
H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}_{-1}\otimes L
and
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}_{-1}\otimes L
has a weakly worse bound.
Item

2
and
Lemma

3.15
follow then.
∎

3.6
The floor/ceiling pseudo instances

Below we summarize our discussions throughout
Section

3
, about how to preprocess valid pseudo instances (
Definition

3.1
).
For ease of reference, we rephrase
Corollaries

3.6
,
3.8
,
3.10
,
3.13
and
3.15
(after minor modifications).

Corollary

3.6
(Restated).

Regarding
First Price Auctions
, the
Price of Anarchy
is at least

\displaystyle{\sf PoA}~\geq~\inf\bigg\{\,\frac{{\sf FPA}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)}{{\sf OPT}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)}\,\,\bigg|\,\bigg.\,(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}~\text{and}~{\sf OPT}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)<+\infty\,\bigg\}.

Lemma

3.8
(Restated).

Given a valid pseudo instance
(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
that has bounded expected auction/optimal
Social Welfares

{\sf FPA}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)\leq{\sf OPT}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)<+\infty
, for any error
\varepsilon\in(0,\,1)
, there is a discretized pseudo instance
(\widetilde{P},\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})
such that
|{\sf PoA}(\widetilde{P},\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})-{\sf PoA}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)|\leq\varepsilon
.

Lemma

3.10
(Restated).

Given a discretized pseudo instance
(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
, there is a translated pseudo instance
(\widetilde{P},\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})
such that
{\sf PoA}(\widetilde{P},\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})\leq{\sf PoA}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
.

Lemma

3.13
(Restated).

Given a translated pseudo instance
(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
, there is a layered pseudo instance
(\widetilde{P},\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})
such that
{\sf PoA}(\widetilde{P},\,\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})\leq{\sf PoA}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
.

Lemma

3.15
(Restated).

Given a layered pseudo instance
(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
, there is a floor/ceiling pseudo instance
\widetilde{H}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow})
such that
{\sf PoA}(\widetilde{H}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})\leq{\sf PoA}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
.

Corollary

3.16
is a refinement of
Corollary

3.6
: Towards a lower bound on the
Price of Anarchy
in
First Price Auctions
, we can restrict our attention to the subspace
(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow})
of
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instances (
Definition

3.14
).

Corollary 3.16

(Lower bound)
.

Regarding
First Price Auctions
, the
Price of Anarchy
is at least

\displaystyle{\sf PoA}~\geq~\inf\bigg\{\,\frac{{\sf FPA}(H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)}{{\sf OPT}(H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)}\,\,\bigg|\,\bigg.\,H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow})~\text{and}~{\sf OPT}(H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)<+\infty\,\bigg\}.

Proof.

For a valid pseudo instance
(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}
considered in
Corollary

3.6
, the
Social Welfares
are bounded
{\sf FPA}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)\leq{\sf OPT}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)<+\infty
.
For any error
\varepsilon\in(0,\,1)
, (
Lemmas

3.8
,
3.10
,
3.13
and
3.15
) there is a
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instance
\widetilde{H}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow})
such that

{\sf PoA}(\widetilde{H}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})~\leq~{\sf PoA}(P,\,\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)+\varepsilon.

Since the error can be arbitrarily small
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
, it has no effect on the
infimum

PoA
bound.
∎

4
Towards the Worst Case Pseudo Instances

Following
Corollary

3.16
, towards a lower bound on the
Price of Anarchy
in
First Price Auctions
, we can concentrate on
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instances
\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow})
.
This section will characterize the worst cases in this search space. More concretely:

•

Section

4.1
presents some prerequisite notions, including the concept of
twin ceiling
pseudo instances
\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
(
Definition

4.2
), which form a subset of the space of
ceiling
pseudo instances
\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}\subsetneq\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
and turn out to be the worst cases.

•

Given an undesirable pseudo instance
\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}))
, we will see that there are four types of possible modifications; see the
4.1
part in
Section

4.1
for details. We give four reductions in
Sections

4.2
,
4.3
,
4.4
and
4.5
, one-to-one dealing with each of these four types.

•

Section

4.6
presents the
11
procedure, which
iteratively
invokes the mentioned four reductions, transforming a given
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instance
\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow})
to a
twin ceiling
pseudo instance
\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
, as desired. Overall, we will use the
potential method
to upper bound the number of invocations.

4.1
Twin ceiling, strong ceiling, jumps, and potentials

This subsection introduces several prerequisite concepts.
But before that, for ease of reference, let us recap the definitions of
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instances (
Definitions

3.1
,
3.7
,
3.9
,
3.12
and
3.14
).

Definition 4.1

(Floor/Ceiling pseudo instances)
.

For a
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instance
H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L
:

•

The
monopolist

H
is a real bidder, who competes with OTHER bidders
\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L
.

•

The
non-monopoly
bidders
\boldsymbol{B}=\{B_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}
(if existential;
n\geq 0
) are real bidders, each of which competes with OTHER bidders
H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}_{-i}\otimes L
.

•

The pseudo bidder
L
competes with ALL bidders
H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L
,
including him/herself
L
.

Using the first-order bid distribution
\mathcal{B}(b):=\prod_{\sigma\in\{H\}\cup[n]\cup\{L\}}B_{\sigma}(b)
, each bidder
\sigma\in\{H\}\cup[n]\cup\{L\}
has the bid-to-value mapping

\varphi_{\sigma}(b)~:=~b+\bigg(\frac{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(b)}{\mathcal{B}(b)}-\frac{B^{\prime}_{\sigma}(b)}{B_{\sigma}(b)}\cdot\mathbbm{1}(\sigma\neq L)\bigg)^{-1}.

This pseudo instance, no matter being
floor

H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}
or being
ceiling

H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
, satisfies
•
‣
4.1
,
•
‣
4.1
, and
•
‣
4.1
.

•

discretization:

This pseudo instance has a
bounded
bid support
b\in[0\equiv\gamma,\,\lambda]\subseteq[0,\,+\infty)
. Regarding some
(m+1)
-piece partition
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}:=[0\equiv\lambda_{0},\,\lambda_{1})\cup[\lambda_{1},\,\lambda_{2})\cup\dots\cup[\lambda_{m},\,\lambda_{m+1}\equiv\lambda]
, for
0\leq m<+\infty
, the bid-to-value mappings
\boldsymbol{\varphi}=\{\varphi_{\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\{H\}\cup[n]\cup\{L\}}
are
piecewise constant
functions and thus, can be represented as an
(n+2)
-to-
(m+1)
bid-to-value table
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=[\phi_{\sigma,\,j}]
for
\sigma\in\{H\}\cup[n]\cup\{L\}
and
j\in[0:\,m]
:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}~:=~\Big[\,\text{$\varphi_{\sigma}(b)\equiv\phi_{\sigma,\,j}$ on every piece $b\in[\lambda_{j},\,\lambda_{j+1})$}\,\Big].

•

monotonicity:

The mappings
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
are increasing over the bid support
b\in[0,\,\lambda]
; thus the table
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=[\phi_{\sigma,\,j}]
is increasing
\phi_{\sigma,\,0}\leq\dots\leq\phi_{\sigma,\,j}\leq\dots\leq\phi_{\sigma,\,m}
in each row
\sigma\in\{H\}\cup[n]\cup\{L\}
.

•

layeredness:

The mappings
\boldsymbol{\varphi}
are ordered
\varphi_{H}(b)\geq\varphi_{1}(b)\geq\dots\geq\varphi_{n}(b)\geq\varphi_{L}(b)
over the bid support
b\in[0,\,\lambda]
; thus the table
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=[\phi_{\sigma,\,j}]
is decreasing
\phi_{H,\,j}\geq\phi_{1,\,j}\geq\dots\geq\phi_{n,\,j}\geq\phi_{L,\,j}
in each column
j\in[0:m]
.

Given these, a pair of
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instances (
Definition

3.14
) are uniquely determined. Namely, the
floor
one
H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}
further satisfies
(
floorness
) that the monopolist
H^{\downarrow}
’s conditional value always takes the
nil value

P^{\downarrow}\equiv 0
,
while the
ceiling
one
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
further satisfies (
ceilingness
) that the monopolist
H^{\uparrow}
’s conditional value always takes the
ceiling value

P^{\uparrow}\equiv\phi_{H,\,0}
.

We notice that possibly there are NO non-monopoly bidders
\boldsymbol{B}=\{B_{i}\}_{i\in[n]}=\emptyset
, or possibly all non-monopoly bidders are “dummies”
B_{i}(b)\equiv 1
on
b\in[0,\,\lambda]
. Suppose so, only the monopolist
H
and the pseudo bidder
L
have effects; then without ambiguity, we can write
H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\cong H\otimes L
.
Indeed, the
twin ceiling
pseudo instances
H^{\uparrow}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
given below (
Definition

4.2
; a visual aid is deferred to
) have this structure and turn out to be the WORST CASES.

Definition 4.2

(Twin ceiling pseudo instances)
.

A
ceiling
pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
is further called
twin ceiling
when it satisfies
•
‣
4.2
and
•
‣
4.2
.

•

absolute ceilingness:

The monopolist
H^{\uparrow}
takes a
constant
bid-to-value mapping
\varphi_{H}(b)\equiv\varphi_{H}(0)
on
b\in[0,\,\lambda]
; thus the row-
H
entries in the bid-to-value table
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=[\phi_{\sigma,\,j}]
are the same
\phi_{H,\,0}=\dots=\phi_{H,\,j}=\dots=\phi_{H,\,m}
. (As before, the
ceiling
conditional value
P^{\uparrow}\equiv\phi_{H,\,0}
.)

•

non-monopoly collapse:

All non-monopoly bidders
i\in[n]
(if existential) each exactly take the pseudo bid-to-value mapping
\varphi_{i}(b)\equiv\varphi_{L}(b)
on
b\in[0,\,\lambda]
; thus in each column
j\in[0:m]
of the bid-to-value table
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=[\phi_{\sigma,\,j}]
, all the non-monopoly entries are the same as the pseudo entry
\phi_{1,\,j}=\dots=\phi_{n,\,j}=\phi_{L,\,j}
.
That is,
20
20

20

Following
Definition

4.1
,
\varphi_{i}(b)\equiv\varphi_{L}(b)
means
B^{\prime}_{i}(b)\big/B_{i}(b)\equiv 0
, which together with the boundary condition at the supremum bid
B_{i}(\lambda)=1
implies that
B_{i}(b)\equiv 1
on
b\in[0,\,\lambda]
.

each non-monopoly bidder
i\in[n]
has no effect
B_{i}(b)\equiv 1
over the bid support
b\in[0,\,\lambda]
.

Since all non-monopoly bidders have no effect, this
twin ceiling
pseudo instance can be written as
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\cong H^{\uparrow}\otimes L
.
Denote by
\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
the space of such pseudo instances, a subset of the space of
ceiling
pseudo instances
\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}\subsetneq\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
.

To convert a
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instance
H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow})
to another
twin ceiling
pseudo instance, a very first question is how to measure its distance “
\mathrm{dist}(H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L,\,\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow})
” to the target space
\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
. Also, a
floor
pseudo instance
H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}
should have a larger distance than the paired
ceiling
pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}
, but just slightly larger since they only differ on the monopolist’s conditional value
P^{\downarrow}\equiv 0
versus
P^{\uparrow}\equiv\phi_{H,\,0}
.

The above intuition guides us to introduce the potential
\Psi(H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
of a
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instance (
Definitions

4.3
and
). As the name “potential” suggests, we will transform such a pseudo instance iteratively until getting a
twin ceiling
pseudo instance
\widetilde{H}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
and use the potential method to bound the number of iterations.

Definition 4.3

(Potentials)
.

Given a pair of
floor
/
ceiling
pseudo instances
H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}
and
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
, consider their
common

(n+2)
-to-
(m+1)
bid-to-value table
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=[\phi_{\sigma,\,j}]
for
\sigma\in\{H\}\cup[n]\cup\{L\}
and
j\in[0:\,m]
(
Definition

4.1
):

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}~=~\Big[\,\text{$\varphi_{\sigma}(b)\equiv\phi_{\sigma,\,j}$ on every piece $b\in[\lambda_{j},\,\lambda_{j+1})$}\,\Big].

An entry
\phi_{\sigma,\,j}
is called
ultra-ceiling
when it
strictly
exceeds the ceiling value
\phi_{\sigma,\,j}>\phi_{H,\,0}\equiv\varphi_{H}(0)
of the monopolist
H
. Then the
ceiling
pseudo instance’s potential counts the ultra-ceiling entries

\Psi(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)~:=~\big|\big\{(\sigma,\,j)\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}:\phi_{\sigma,\,j}>\phi_{H,\,0}\big\}\big|.\hskip 9.95863pt

In contrast, the
floor
pseudo instance’s potential adds an extra ONE (which accounts for the nil conditional value
P^{\downarrow}\equiv 0
rather than
P^{\uparrow}\equiv\phi_{H,\,0}
):

\Psi(H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)~:=~\Psi(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)~+~1.~~\hskip 34.71239pt

Lemma

4.4
summarizes several basic properties about the potentials.

Lemma 4.4

(Potentials)
.

The following hold:

1.

A floor/ceiling pseudo instance
H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow})
has a bounded potential

0\leq\Psi(H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)\leq|\boldsymbol{\Phi}|<+\infty
.

2.

A floor pseudo instance
H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}
has a nonzero potential
\Psi(H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)\geq 1
.

3.

A ceiling pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
has a zero potential
\Psi(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)=0
iff it satisfies

‣
4.2
.

Proof.

\Psi(H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)\leq\big(|\boldsymbol{\Phi}|-1\big)+1=|\boldsymbol{\Phi}|
. Namely, the potential cannot counts the ceiling value
\phi_{H,\,0}
itself, but may add an extra ONE for the nil conditional value
P^{\downarrow}\equiv 0
.

Item

.

) all the row-

.

Because the table
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=[\phi_{\sigma,\,j}]

Definition 4.5

(Jumps)
.

For a

denotes the

•

\sigma^{*}:=\max\big\{\sigma\in\{H<1<\dots<n<L\}:\phi_{\sigma,\,j^{*}}>\varphi_{H,\,0}\big\}

.

(Jumps)

Definition 4.7

(Strong ceiling pseudo instances)
.

A

) before the jump bid

, each non-monopoly bidder

(if existential) exactly takes the pseudo bid-to-value mapping

; therefore in each before-jump column

That is, before the jump bid

, each non-monopoly bidder

and thus has no effect.

Following

,
\varphi_{i}(b)=\varphi_{L}(b)

on

.

Denote by

the space of such pseudo instances, an intermediate class between the

Lemma 4.8

(Twin ceiling pseudo instances)
.

A strong ceiling pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf strong}^{\uparrow}
is further a twin ceiling pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\cong H^{\uparrow}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
iff one of the three equivalent conditions holds: (i) It satisfies
•
‣
4.2
. (ii) Its potential is zero
\Psi(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)=0
. (iii) Its jump bid is the supremum bid
\lambda^{*}=\lambda
.

4.2
Slice
: From floor to ceiling

This subsection presents the
reduction (see
and
for its description and a visual aid), which transforms (
Definition

4.1
) a
floor
pseudo instance
H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}
into a
ceiling
pseudo instance
\widetilde{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
.

Lemma

4.9
summarizes performance guarantees of the
reduction. (The intuition of this reduction might be obscure. In brief, it generalizes the ideas behind the
reduction and the
reduction from
Section

3
.)

Lemma 4.9

(
Slice
;
)
.

Under reduction
\widetilde{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}\leftarrow\text{\tt Slice}(H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
:

1.

The output is a ceiling pseudo instance
\widetilde{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
.

2.

The potential strictly decreases
\Psi(\widetilde{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})\leq\Psi(H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)-1
.

3.

A (weakly) worse bound is yielded
{\sf PoA}(\widetilde{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})\leq{\sf PoA}(H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
.

Proof.

See
for a visual aid.
Without loss of generality, we assume that the input bound is strictly less than one
{\sf PoA}(H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)<1
; otherwise, the input
floor
pseudo instance
H^{\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L
cannot be (one of) the worst cases.
For brevity, let
[N]\equiv\{H\}\cup[n]\cup\{L\}
.

The
reduction
(Line
) considers a spectrum
\big\{\,H^{(t)\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}^{(t)}\otimes L^{(t)}:0\leq t<\lambda\,\big\}
of
pseudo instances,
then (Line
) takes the
PoA
-minimizer
t^{*}\in[0,\,\lambda)
,
and then (Line
) outputs the
ceiling
pseudo instance
H^{(t^{*})\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}^{(t^{*})}\otimes L^{(t^{*})}
given by the
t^{*}
.

Our analysis relies on
A
. But we defer its proof to
Item

3
to ease readability.

.

The minimizer
t^{*}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}\big\{\,{\sf PoA}(H^{(t)\downarrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}^{(t)}\otimes L^{(t)}):0\leq t<\lambda\,\big\}
, breaking ties in favor of the smallest one among all the alternatives (Line
), is well defined.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{O}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big(1-\prod_{\sigma\in[N]}\big(1-\big(1-B_{\sigma}^{(t)}(0)\big)\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v<\phi_{\sigma,\,m}^{(t)})\big)\Big)\cdot\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big(\sum_{\sigma\in[N]}\big(1-B_{\sigma}^{(t)}(0)\big)\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v<\phi_{\sigma,\,m}^{(t)})\Big)\cdot\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\sigma\in[N]}\phi_{\sigma,\,0}\cdot\tfrac{B_{\sigma}^{\prime}(0)}{B_{\sigma}(0)}\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)-\big(1-\mathcal{B}(0)\big).\qed

.

The derivative

\displaystyle=-\big(1-\mathcal{B}(0)\big)-\sum_{\sigma\in[N]}\Big(\tfrac{B^{\prime}_{\sigma}(0)}{B_{\sigma}(0)}\cdot\int_{0}^{\phi_{\sigma,\,0}}\mathcal{V}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v\Big).

is increasing.

We thus conclude that
{\sf PoA}({\ref{slice_output}})=\frac{\mathfrak{F}(0)+\Delta_{{\sf FPA}}}{\mathfrak{O}(0)+\Delta_{{\sf OPT}}}\leq\frac{\mathfrak{F}(0)}{\mathfrak{O}(0)}={\sf PoA}({\ref{slice_minimizer}})\leq{\sf PoA}({\ref{slice_input}})
.
I.e., the
ceiling

yields a (weakly) worse bound.
Item

3
follows then.
This finishes the proof.
∎

4.3
Collapse
: From ceiling to strong ceiling

Following the previous subsection, we are able to handle
floor
pseudo instances
\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}
.
Therefore,
we can restrict our attention to
ceiling
pseudo instances
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
.
Indeed, every
ceiling
pseudo instance can be transformed into a
strong ceiling
pseudo instance, through the
reduction (see
and
for its description and a visual aid). For ease of reference, let us rephrase the definition of
strong ceiling
pseudo instances.

Definition

4.7
(Restated).

A ceiling pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
from
Definition

4.1
is further called strong ceiling when (
non-monopoly collapse
) each non-monopoly bidder
i\in[n]
(if existential) exactly takes the pseudo bid-to-value mapping
\varphi_{i}(b)\equiv\varphi_{L}(b)
before the jump bid
b\in[0,\,\lambda^{*})
; therefore in each before-jump column
j\in[0:j^{*}-1]
of the bid-to-value table
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=[\phi_{\sigma,\,j}]
, all of the non-monopoly entries are the same as the pseudo entry
\phi_{1,\,j}=\dots=\phi_{n,\,j}=\phi_{L,\,j}
.
That is, before the jump bid
b\in[0,\,\lambda^{*})
, each non-monopoly bidder
i\in[n]
has a constant bid distribution
B_{i}(b)=B_{i}(\lambda^{*})
and thus has no effect.

Intuition
.

Regarding a ceiling pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
, in the sense of
Lemma

4.10
, the optimal
Social Welfare
formula
{\sf OPT}(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
turns out to be irrelevant to the non-ultra-ceiling entries
\phi_{\sigma,\,j}\leq\phi_{H,\,0}
in the bid-to-value table
\boldsymbol{\Phi}=\big[\phi_{\sigma,\,j}\big]
(except the ceiling value
\phi_{H,\,0}
itself).
Conceivably, we would modify those non-ultra-ceiling entries
\phi_{\sigma,\,j}\leq\phi_{H,\,0}
to minimize the auction
Social Welfare

{\sf FPA}(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
, while preserving
•
‣
4.1
etc.

Lemma 4.10

(
Social Welfares
)
.

Following
Lemma

3.11
, for a ceiling pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L
, the expected optimal
Social Welfare
can be written as

\displaystyle{\sf OPT}(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)~=~\int_{0}^{+\infty}\bigg(1-\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\prod_{(\sigma,\,j)\,\in\,\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}}\Big(1-\omega_{\sigma,\,j}\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v<\phi_{\sigma,\,j})\Big)\bigg)\cdot\mathrm{d}v.

where the subtable
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}\subseteq\boldsymbol{\Phi}
includes all of the ultra-ceiling entries
\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{*}\supseteq\big\{(\sigma,\,j)\in\boldsymbol{\Phi}:\phi_{\sigma,\,j}>\phi_{H,\,0}\big\}
but otherwise is arbitrary.

Proof.

Following
Lemma

3.11
(with
4.1

P^{\uparrow}\equiv\phi_{H,\,0}
), the optimal
Social Welfare
is given by
{\sf OPT}(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\big(1-\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\prod_{(\sigma,\,j)\,\in\,\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\big(1-\omega_{\sigma,\,j}\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v<\phi_{\sigma,\,j})\big)\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}v
.
Each non-ultra-ceiling entry
\phi_{\sigma,\,j}\leq\phi_{H,\,0}
has no effect because of the leading
indicator
term
\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0})
.
∎

The
reduction implements the above intuition, and its performance guarantees are summarized in
Lemma

4.11
.

Lemma 4.11

(
Collapse
;
)
.

Under reduction
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}\leftarrow\text{\tt Collapse}(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
:

1.

The output is a strong ceiling pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf strong}^{\uparrow}\subsetneq\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}
; the monopolist
H^{\uparrow}
is unmodified.

2.

The potential keeps the same
\Psi(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})=\Psi(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
.

3.

A (weakly) worse bound is yielded
{\sf PoA}(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L})\leq{\sf PoA}(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
.

Proof.

See
for a visual aid.
For brevity, let
[N]\equiv\{H\}\cup[n]\cup\{L\}
.

Item

1
.

By construction, the
non-monopoly bidders
\widetilde{B}_{i}(b)\equiv\max\big(B_{i}(b),\,B_{i}(\lambda^{*})\big)
for
i\in[n]
and the
pseudo bidder
\widetilde{L}(b)\equiv L(b)\cdot\prod_{i\in[n]}\big(B_{i}(b)\big/\widetilde{B}_{i}(b)\big)
are well defined (Line
). Moreover, the monopolist
H^{\uparrow}
is invariant.

After the jump bid
b\in(\lambda^{*},\,\lambda]
, everything keeps the same.
And before the jump bid
b\in[0,\,\lambda^{*}]
, the first-order bid distribution
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}(b)=\prod_{\sigma\in[N]}\widetilde{B}_{\sigma}(b)=\prod_{\sigma\in[N]}B_{\sigma}(b)=\mathcal{B}(b)
keeps the same;
so the pseudo mapping
\varphi_{L}(b)=b+\frac{\mathcal{B}(b)}{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(b)}
and the monopolist
H^{\uparrow}
’s mapping
\varphi_{H}(b)=b+(\frac{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(b)}{\mathcal{B}(b)}-\frac{H^{\prime}(b)}{H(b)})^{-1}
are invariant (cf.
).
Moreover, each non-monopoly bidder
i\in[n]

does
have a constant bid distributions
\widetilde{B}_{i}(b)=B_{i}(\lambda^{*})
for
b\in[0,\,\lambda^{*}]
(
Definition

4.7
).
Hence, the

H^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}
satisfies
•
‣
4.1
,
•
‣
4.1
,
•
‣
4.1
, and
4.1

à la the
ceiling

H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L
(
Definition

4.1
) and additionally satisfies
4.3
(
Definition

4.7
), being a
strong ceiling
pseudo instance
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf strong}^{\uparrow}
.

Item

1
follows then.

Item

2
.

The potential of a
ceiling
pseudo instance
\widehat{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widehat{L}
counts all the ultra-ceiling entries
\Psi(\text{ceiling})=\big|\big\{(\sigma,\,j)\in\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}:\widehat{\phi}_{\sigma,\,j}>\widehat{\phi}_{H,\,0}\big\}\big|
in the bid-to-value table
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}=\big[\widehat{\phi}_{\sigma,\,j}\big]
(
Definition

4.3
).
We do consider two
ceiling
pseudo instances (
Item

1
) and need to verify that the

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}

\boldsymbol{\Phi}
.

The

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}
(Line
and
) descends the non-monopoly entries

to the
lower
pseudo entries

(
4.3
) in before-jump columns

.
All of these entries

(
•
‣
4.1
and
Definition

) are non-ultra-ceiling regardless of the reduction:
[\widetilde{\phi}_{i,\,j}=\phi_{i,\,L}]\leq[\phi_{i,\,j}]\leq[\phi_{H,\,j}=\phi_{H,\,0}]

So, the

H^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}
keeps the same potential
\Psi({\ref{collapse_output}})=\Psi({\ref{collapse_input}})
.

Item

3
.

We would show that compared to the

H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L

(weakly) decreases the auction

Auction
Social Welfares
.

Following

(with
\gamma\equiv 0
and

by

), the
has an auction

{\sf FPA}({\ref{collapse_input}})=\phi_{H,\,0}\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)+\sum_{\sigma\in[N]}\big(\int_{0}^{\lambda}\varphi_{\sigma}(b)\cdot\frac{B^{\prime}_{\sigma}(b)}{B_{\sigma}(b)}\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b\big)
.
As mentioned (

b\in(\lambda^{*},\,\lambda]

(ii) Everything about the monopolist

We can formulate the output auction

likewise and, by concentrating on the changed parts (namely

and

\displaystyle{\sf FPA}({\ref{collapse_output}})

\displaystyle~=~\sum_{\sigma\neq H}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\lambda^{*}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\sigma}(b)\cdot\frac{\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{\sigma}(b)}{\widetilde{B}_{\sigma}(b)}\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b\bigg)\hskip 0.56917pt~+~\mathrm{const}

\displaystyle~=~\int_{0}^{\lambda^{*}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{L}(b)\cdot\frac{\widetilde{L}^{\prime}(b)}{\widetilde{L}(b)}\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b\hskip 38.41139pt~+~\mathrm{const}

(C1)

\displaystyle~=~\int_{0}^{\lambda^{*}}\varphi_{L}(b)\cdot\bigg(\sum_{\sigma\neq H}\frac{B^{\prime}_{\sigma}(b)}{B_{\sigma}(b)}\bigg)\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b~+~\mathrm{const}

(C2)

\displaystyle~\leq~\sum_{\sigma\neq H}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\lambda^{*}}\varphi_{\sigma}(b)\cdot\frac{B^{\prime}_{\sigma}(b)}{B_{\sigma}(b)}\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b\bigg)~+~\mathrm{const}~=~{\sf FPA}({\ref{collapse_input}}).

(C3)

(

):

\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{i}\big/\widetilde{B}_{i}=0
for each

(

)
before the jump bid

(

The pseudo mapping is the dominated mapping

4.4
Halve
: From strong ceiling towards twin ceiling, under pseudo jumps

Following the previous two subsections, we are able to deal with
floor
/
ceiling
but
non strong ceiling
pseudo instances
(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{\sf strong}^{\uparrow}))
.
As a consequence, it remains to deal with
strong ceiling
but
non twin ceiling
pseudo instances
H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf strong}^{\uparrow}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow})

For ease of reference, below we rephrase the observations from
Section

4.1
on such pseudo instances.

Lemmas

4.4
,
4.6
and
4.8
(Restated).

For a strong ceiling but non twin ceiling pseudo instance

1.

It violates
•
‣
4.2
, i.e., its potential is nonzero

.

2.

The
4.5

(\sigma^{*},\,j^{*})\in(\{H\}\cup[n]\cup\{L\})\times[m]
exists and cannot be in column
0
.

3.

The jump bid as the index
j^{*}\in[m]
partition bid
\lambda^{*}\equiv\lambda_{j^{*}}\in(0,\,\lambda)
neither can be the nil bid
\lambda_{0}\equiv 0
nor can be the supremum bid

.

In particular, the current subsection deals with the case of a
pseudo jump

\sigma^{*}=L
, using the

reduction (see

and
for its description and a visual aid).

Reduction
\textup{\text{\tt Halve}}(H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)

Input:
A

but

.

input

A

/

\widetilde{H}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\downarrow}\cup\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow})
.

output

1.

Define the

H^{\sf L\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}^{\sf L}\otimes L^{\sf L}

ceiling

given by
B_{\sigma}^{\sf L}(b)\equiv\min\big(B_{\sigma}(b)\big/B_{\sigma}(\lambda^{*}),\,1\big)
for

given by

for

Return
the

\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}\big\{\,{\sf PoA}(\text{left}),~{\sf PoA}(\text{right})\,\big\}

Return
breaking ties in favor of the

Figure 10:

reduction

Intuition
.

As

Lemma 4.12

(

;
Figure

for a visual aid. The

The potential of a

\displaystyle{\sf FPA}({\ref{halve_left}})\hskip 9.67383pt

\displaystyle~=~\Big(\phi_{H,\,0}\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)+\sum_{\sigma\in[N]}\int_{0}^{\lambda^{*}}\varphi_{\sigma}(b)\cdot\frac{B^{\prime}_{\sigma}(b)}{B_{\sigma}(b)}\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\mathcal{B}(\lambda^{*})}.

\displaystyle~=~\sum_{\sigma\in[N]}\int_{0}^{\lambda-\lambda^{*}}\varphi_{\sigma}^{\sf R}(b)\cdot\frac{{B_{\sigma}^{\sf R}}^{\prime}(b)}{B_{\sigma}^{\sf R}(b)}\cdot\mathcal{B}^{\sf R}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b

\displaystyle~=~\sum_{\sigma\in[N]}\int_{\lambda^{*}}^{\lambda}\varphi_{\sigma}(b)\cdot\frac{B^{\prime}_{\sigma}(b)}{B_{\sigma}(b)}\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b-\int_{\lambda^{*}}^{\lambda}\lambda^{*}\cdot\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b\hskip 28.45274pt

Fact A
.

\displaystyle\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}

\displaystyle~=~(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)+\sum_{j=0}^{j^{*}-1}\int_{\lambda_{j}}^{\lambda_{j+1}}\Big(\big(\tfrac{\phi^{*}}{\varphi_{L}(b)-b}-\tfrac{\phi^{*}-\varphi_{L}(b)}{\phi^{*}-b}\big)

\displaystyle\phantom{~=~(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)\sum_{j=0}^{j^{*}-1}\int_{\lambda_{j}}^{\lambda_{j+1}}\Big(}-\big(\tfrac{\phi_{H,\,0}}{\varphi_{L}(b)-b}-\tfrac{\phi_{H,\,0}-\varphi_{L}(b)}{\phi_{H,\,0}-b}\big)\Big)\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b

(restate)

\displaystyle~=~(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)+\int_{0}^{\lambda_{j^{*}}}\Big(\tfrac{\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0}}{\varphi_{L}(b)-b}+\big(\tfrac{\phi_{H,\,0}-\varphi_{L}(b)}{\phi_{H,\,0}-b}-\tfrac{\phi^{*}-\varphi_{L}(b)}{\phi^{*}-b}\big)\Big)\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b\hskip 55.48277pt~~\;\;

\displaystyle~\leq~(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)+\int_{0}^{\lambda_{j^{*}}}\phantom{\Big(}\tfrac{\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0}}{\varphi_{L}(b)-b}\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b

(A1)

\displaystyle~=~(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathcal{B}(0)+\int_{0}^{\lambda_{j^{*}}}(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathcal{B}^{\prime}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b\phantom{\bigg.}

(A2)

\displaystyle~=~(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathcal{B}(\lambda_{j^{*}}).\phantom{\bigg.}

(
A1
): The dropped term
\leq 0
(
Definitions

4.5
and
3.3
;
\phi^{*}\equiv\phi_{\sigma^{*},\,j^{*}}>\phi_{H,\,0}\geq\varphi_{L}(b)>b
).

(
A2
): The pseudo mapping
\varphi_{L}=b+\mathcal{B}\big/\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
(
Definition

3.1
).

The
auction
Social Welfare
change
\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}\equiv{\sf FPA}({\ref{AD_input}})-{\sf FPA}({\ref{AD_descend}})
is at least

\displaystyle\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}

\displaystyle~=~\int_{\lambda_{j^{*}}}^{\lambda_{j^{*}+1}}\Big(\big(\tfrac{\phi^{*}}{\varphi_{L}(b)-b}-\tfrac{\phi^{*}-\varphi_{L}(b)}{\phi^{*}-b}\big)-\big(\tfrac{\phi_{H,\,0}}{\varphi_{L}(b)-b}-\tfrac{\phi_{H,\,0}-\varphi_{L}(b)}{\phi_{H,\,0}-b}\big)\Big)\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b

(restate)

\displaystyle~=~\int_{\lambda_{j^{*}}}^{\lambda_{j^{*}+1}}\Big(\big(\tfrac{\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0}}{\varphi_{L}(b)-b}-\tfrac{\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0}}{\phi^{*}-b}\big)+\big(\tfrac{\phi_{H,\,0}-\varphi_{L}(b)}{\phi_{H,\,0}-b}-\tfrac{\phi_{H,\,0}-\varphi_{L}(b)}{\phi^{*}-b}\big)\Big)\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b\hskip 78.52945pt~~

\displaystyle~\geq~\int_{\lambda_{j^{*}}}^{\lambda_{j^{*}+1}}\phantom{\Big(}\big(\tfrac{\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0}}{\varphi_{L}(b)-b}-\tfrac{\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0}}{\phi^{*}-b}\big)\cdot\mathcal{B}(b)\cdot\mathrm{d}b

(D1)

\displaystyle~=~\int_{\lambda_{j^{*}}}^{\lambda_{j^{*}+1}}~~\;(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot B^{\prime}_{\sigma^{*}}(b)\cdot\tfrac{\mathcal{B}(b)}{B_{\sigma^{*}}(b)}\cdot\mathrm{d}b

(D2)

\displaystyle~\geq~\int_{\lambda_{j^{*}}}^{\lambda_{j^{*}+1}}~~\;(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot B^{\prime}_{\sigma^{*}}(b)\cdot\tfrac{\mathcal{B}(\lambda_{j^{*}})}{B_{\sigma^{*}}(\lambda_{j^{*}})}\cdot\mathrm{d}b

(D3)

\displaystyle~=~(\phi^{*}-\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\big(\tfrac{B_{\sigma^{*}}(\lambda_{j^{*}+1})}{B_{\sigma^{*}}(\lambda_{j^{*}})}-1\big)\cdot\mathcal{B}(\lambda_{j^{*}})~\geq~0.

(
D1
): The dropped term
\geq 0
(

4.5
and
3.3
;
\phi^{*}\equiv\phi_{\sigma^{*},\,j^{*}}>\phi_{H,\,0}\geq\varphi_{L}(b)>b
).

(

):
\phi^{*}\equiv\phi_{\sigma^{*},\,j^{*}}=b+\big(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\big/\mathcal{B}-B^{\prime}_{\sigma^{*}}\big/B_{\sigma^{*}}\big)^{-1}
(given a
4.5

\sigma^{*}\neq L
) and
\varphi_{L}=b+\mathcal{B}\big/\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
.

(

):
\mathcal{B}\big/B_{\sigma^{*}}=\prod_{\sigma\in[N]\setminus\{\sigma^{*}\}}B_{\sigma}
is an increasing CDF.

Combining the above two equations and applying the substitution
\omega^{*}\equiv\omega_{\sigma^{*},\,j^{*}}=1-\frac{B_{\sigma^{*}}(\lambda_{j^{*}})}{B_{\sigma^{*}}(\lambda_{j^{*}+1})}
(
Lemma

3.11
), we deduce that
\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}\big/\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}\leq(1-\omega^{*})/\omega^{*}
.

The
optimum change
\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}\equiv{\sf OPT}({\ref{AD_ascend}})-{\sf OPT}({\ref{AD_input}})
is equal to

\displaystyle\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}

\displaystyle~=~\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big(\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\big(1-\omega^{*}\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v<\phi^{*})\big)-\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi^{*})\big)\cdot\mathcal{I}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v

(restate)

\displaystyle~=~\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big(\big(1-\omega^{*}\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v<\phi^{*})\big)-\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi^{*})\Big)\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathcal{I}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v\hskip 95.3169pt~~

\displaystyle~=~\big(1-\omega^{*}\big)\cdot\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathbbm{1}\big(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0}\big)\cdot\mathbbm{1}\big(v<\phi^{*}\big)\cdot\mathcal{I}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v.

The second step:
\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi^{*})\equiv\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi^{*})
because the jump value
\phi^{*}\equiv\phi_{\sigma^{*},\,j^{*}}>\phi_{H,\,0}
.

The last step:
\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi^{*})\equiv 1-\mathbbm{1}(v<\phi^{*})
.

The
optimum change
\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}\equiv{\sf OPT}({\ref{AD_input}})-{\sf OPT}({\ref{AD_descend}})
is given by

\displaystyle\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}

\displaystyle~=~\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big(\Big(1-\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\big(1-\omega^{*}\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v<\phi^{*})\big)\cdot\mathcal{I}(v)\Big)-\Big(1-\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathcal{I}(v)\Big)\Big)\cdot\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle~=~\omega^{*}\cdot\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathbbm{1}(v\geq\phi_{H,\,0})\cdot\mathbbm{1}(v<\phi^{*})\cdot\mathcal{I}(v)\cdot\mathrm{d}v.

We thus conclude that
\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}\big/\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}=(1-\omega^{*})/\omega^{*}\geq\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}\big/\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}
, which implies
A
.
∎

Assume the opposite to
Item

3
: Both of the
/
candidates
\overline{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\overline{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\overline{L}
and
\underline{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\underline{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\underline{L}
yield strictly larger bounds than the

H^{\uparrow}\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L
. That is,

\displaystyle{\sf PoA}({\ref{AD_ascend}})\;\;\;>~{\sf PoA}({\ref{AD_input}})

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\frac{{\sf FPA}({\ref{AD_input}})+\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}}{{\sf OPT}({\ref{AD_input}})+\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}}~>~\frac{{\sf FPA}({\ref{AD_input}})}{{\sf OPT}({\ref{AD_input}})},

\displaystyle{\sf PoA}({\ref{AD_descend}})~>~{\sf PoA}({\ref{AD_input}})

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\frac{{\sf FPA}({\ref{AD_input}})-\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}}{{\sf OPT}({\ref{AD_input}})-\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}}~>~\frac{{\sf FPA}({\ref{AD_input}})}{{\sf OPT}({\ref{AD_input}})}.

Rearranging both equations gives
\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}\big/\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}\,>\,{\sf PoA}({\ref{AD_input}})\,>\,\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf FPA}}\big/\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}
(notice that the denominators
\overline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}},\,\underline{\Delta}_{{\sf OPT}}>0
), which contradicts
A
.
Refute our assumption: At least one candidate between
\overline{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\overline{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\overline{L}
and
\underline{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\underline{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\underline{L}
yields a (weakly) worse bound.
Item

3
follows.

This finishes the proof of
Lemma

4.13
.
∎

4.6
The main algorithm: From floor/ceiling to twin ceiling

This subsection presents the
11
procedure (see

11
for its description), which transforms a

/

into a
twin ceiling

\widetilde{H}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
(

4.2
).
This
11

(

Overall, through the potential method (

finite

twin ceiling
output
\widetilde{H}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
afterward.

.

\widetilde{\Psi}\leq\Psi-1
.

Lemma 4.14

(
Main
;
Figure

11
)
.

Under procedure
\widetilde{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{L}\leftarrow\text{\tt Main}(H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)

1.

It terminates in at most
(1+2\Psi^{*})

(

\widetilde{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}

A (weakly) worse bound is yielded
{\sf PoA}(\widetilde{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{L})\leq{\sf PoA}(H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L)
.

Input:

A
floor
/

Definition

4.1

Output:

A

\widetilde{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widetilde{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
.

input

Definition

4.2

Remark:

“

H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\,\leftarrow\,\widetilde{H}\otimes\widetilde{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widetilde{L}

1.

While

\big\{H\otimes\boldsymbol{B}\otimes L\notin\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}\big\}

2.

(

)

(

.

.

The
Main
procedure

Proof.

It suffices to show
Item

1
. Suppose so,
Items

2
and
3
follow directly from the termination condition for the
1

1
) and a combination of
Lemmas

4.9
,
4.11
,
4.12
and
4.13
.

Item

1
.

We can divide all of the four reductions into two types.

•

Type-1:
,
10
, and
.

Such a reduction decreases the potential
\widetilde{\Psi}\leq\Psi-1
. Therefore, such reductions in total can be invoked at most “the
11
potential” many times. (Recall
Lemma

4.4
that a
twin ceiling
pseudo instance
\widehat{H}^{\uparrow}\otimes\widehat{\boldsymbol{B}}\otimes\widehat{L}\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf twin}^{\uparrow}
, which triggers the termination condition for the
1
loop, has a zero potential.) Formally, we have
\#\big[\textbf{Type-1}\big]\,\leq\,\Psi^{*}
.

•

Type-2:
.

This reduction transforms a
ceiling
but
non strong ceiling
pseudo instance
\in(\mathbb{B}_{\sf valid}^{\uparrow}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{\sf strong}^{\uparrow})
that violates
4.7
into a
strong ceiling
pseudo instance
\in\mathbb{B}_{\sf strong}^{\uparrow}
that satisfies
4.7
. Therefore, between any two invocations of this reduction, (Line
4
) at least one invocation of another
Type-1
reduction is required. Formally, we have
\#\big[\textbf{Type-2}\big]\,\leq\,\#\big[\textbf{Type-1}\big]+1\,\leq\,\Psi^{*}+1
.

Hence, the
11
procedure terminates in at most
\#\big[\textbf{Type-1}\big]+\#\big[\textbf{Type-2}\big]\leq 2\Psi^{*}+1
iterations.

1
follows then.
